## Codex Review (astra-review)

Reviewed all nine plans against checkout `b94d776b`. Production code, tests and documentation match `dbf1e6c4`. Existing ancestor-helper, ancestor-cost, navbar-cost and child-link suites passed: **398 tests**, Python 3.12.3 / Django 6.0.10. This validates the baseline, not the proposed implementation.

## 06.48-01

**Summary:** A strong baseline-first plan, with one weakness in how failed scenarios can become accepted snapshots.

**Strengths**

- Freezing resolved outcomes matches the actual visibility contract: permission denial takes precedence over state denial, and disabled controls lose their URL. See [visibility.py:12](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views/lib/view/visibility.py:12).
- Preserving authenticated inactive sessions avoids conflating two distinct actor paths.

**Concerns**

- **MEDIUM — Unexpected exceptions can become passing baseline cells.** [Plan 01:179](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md:179) permits any exception to become `raises:<class>`. A broken fixture or URL setup can therefore count toward 1,178 cells without exercising its control. The existing [rendered_data helper:137](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:137) propagates such failures.

**Suggestions**

- Reject `raises:*` during capture unless the scenario explicitly specifies that exception.
- Require a successfully rendered positive case for each control/composition/target family, alongside denied-page cases.

**Risk assessment:** **MEDIUM** until capture distinguishes expected outcomes from fixture failures.

## 06.48-02

**Summary:** The proposed composition fits the existing authorization machinery, but an unresolved contract decision prevents unconditional execution approval.

**Strengths**

- Keeping provider `view=` values unchanged protects both query costs and trace ownership: [Guardian ancestors:61](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:61) selects the checker, and [line 94](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:94) selects the trace owner.
- Delegating nested-create permission to `cv_create_has_access` avoids duplicating its existing immediate-parent gate at [Guardian views:339](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:339).

**Concerns**

- **HIGH — L1 remains an unapproved exception to D-28/D-33.** The latest [CONTEXT.md:130](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-CONTEXT.md:130) explicitly says these interpretations are awaiting confirmation. Concrete case: the parent create hook denies, but the page’s own create allows; [Guardian mixins:328](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:328) overwrites the button’s denial. Preservation is defensible, but it does not satisfy an unconditional promise that the destination hook governs every shown control.

**Suggestions**

- Record the maintainer’s resolution before treating L1—and the related pending interpretations—as settled execution inputs. This requires reconciling requirements, not redesigning the implementation.

**Risk assessment:** **HIGH for readiness**, owing to the unresolved contract conflict; no new request-authorization bypass identified.

## 06.48-03

**Summary:** The unit-test plan meaningfully tests the difficult parts of the private function.

**Strengths**

- Using a create target with different nearest/higher permissions tests the actual selection rule in [ancestors.py:397](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:397).
- Ordered provider assertions can reuse a real recording fixture at [test_ancestor_helpers.py:131](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:131), rather than testing only final booleans.

**Concerns**

- **LOW — The cache test proves a narrower property than its name implies.** Re-fetching the user and checking only attributes containing `"cv"` would not detect a module-level cache keyed by user identity. Existing provider caching is explicitly view-local at [Guardian ancestors:68](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:68).

**Suggestions**

- Add a small test that changes a mocked permission answer between two identical calls using the same target, user and ancestor objects. Both calls must reach the permission seam.

**Risk assessment:** **LOW**, subject to resolving plan 02’s pending decisions.

**Concerns**

- No additional source-backed defect identified. The intended order change and its configuration-error consequences are explicitly accounted for.

**Suggestions**

- Retain separate assertions for anonymous/inactive denial and custom own-access denial when restructuring the cost test.

**Risk assessment:** **LOW**; the principal behavioral and performance changes are named and testable.

[CONTEXT.md:147](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-CONTEXT.md:147) acknowledges the narrowed wording.

**Risk assessment:** **MEDIUM**, principally from contract wording rather than the proposed lookup mechanism.

## 06.48-06

**Summary:** The remaining consumers are covered, including a suitably isolated comparison between rendering and request enforcement.

**Strengths**

- Keeping the filter permission-only preserves its present responsibility at [template tags:649](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views/templatetags/crud_views.py:649).
- Separating enforcement calls from rendering calls is necessary: core enforcement invokes state in [base.py:1321](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:1321), before page construction.

**Concerns**

- **LOW — The L1 gate-count description overgeneralizes the parent-button case.** Plan 02 says both parent and sibling collisions run two gates. A sibling button currently passes `None` at [buttons.py:301](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:301); nested Guardian create returns immediately at [Guardian views:382](/home/alex/projects/alex/django-crud-views/src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:382). Consequently, that sibling collision runs only the overwrite’s gate.

**Suggestions**

- State “two gates for the parent-button case; one for the sibling collision.”
- Add an isolated sibling-key-collision test rather than relying exclusively on the parent-button L1 cells.

**Risk assessment:** **LOW**, with the L1 approval dependency already identified above.

**Suggestions**

- Reset recordings and isolate each control invocation, or record an explicit control identifier. Include a negative control demonstrating that removing one invocation fails its own assertion.

**Risk assessment:** **MEDIUM** until the test proves the advertised coverage.

## OpenCode Review (grok-review)

I'll verify the plans against the source, starting with the call sites and hooks they claim to change.
## 06.48-01

The baseline plan is the right Wave 0, and the cell arithmetic matches the dimensions it lists (504 + 40 = 544; 634 more = 1178). L1’s four answers follow the current overwrite at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:328-356` plus case 4 at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:382-385`. The inactive Guardian page cells do not freeze the control the issue asks for.

**Strengths**
- Capture-before-edit, read-only committed test, and `source_revision` tied to `dbf1e6c4` make “same rendered result” checkable.
- Seven-segment ids, duplicate refusal, and exact key counts close the earlier collision and under-count holes.
- L1 actors `near-change` / `far-change` actually discriminate list-page overwrite from detail-page parent create.

**Concerns**
- MEDIUM: Task 02 records `page:<status>` whenever the page is not 200. An authenticated inactive user is refused by `_guardian_actor_response` before any control is built (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:56-58`). Every Guardian list/detail/update/delete/create cell for `inactive` therefore freezes admission, not the create control, home button, parent button, sibling button, row action, card action, or filter. The completeness check only requires one inactive cell to differ from anonymous, which `page:403` versus a login redirect already satisfies. AC-2 for those controls is then unproven for inactive.
- LOW: `core-guardian` / `inactive` is hard-required to be `disabled:permission` before capture. That matches `cv_has_access`’s inactive return (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:382-383`) only if the cell is actually rendered in `disable` mode. A scaffold miss becomes a stop, which is safe but brittle.

**Suggestions**
- For inactive control cells, run the guard as an actor who may open the page, then swap `request.user` to the inactive user before reading the spied context. Keep a separate cell for page admission.
- Do not treat `page:403` as the frozen rendered result of the control.

**Risk Assessment:** MEDIUM. The matrix is otherwise a sound gate, but the inactive Guardian page slice does not measure the thing later plans must not change.

## 06.48-02

The tracer matches the code that exists. Navbar child items call `_cv_child_link_has_access` then the rendering adapter (`src/crud_views/lib/navbar.py:84-87`). The list-page create branch really does call `cv_get_parent_object` and stamp both fields (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:343-356`). Keeping that condition line-for-line is what preserves L1. The spy-loop rewrite matches `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:150-152`.

**Strengths**
- New module plus call-time `ancestors._immediate_parent_access` keeps D-14 as an empty diff and stays visible to `parent_spy`.
- `_higher_ancestor_gates` follows `_authorize_ancestors`: static pair, actor admission, then per-level auth (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:473-534`). “No level to gate” before the actor rule is the right reading of D-16.
- C2 is real: `test_reached_policy_errors_propagate` renders empty for `allowed=False` only because own denial returns first (`src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:530-531`).

**Concerns**
- LOW: Removing the list-page `try`/`except` is correct for D-04, but `test_list_create_control_late_parent_failure_skips_state_hook` currently expects `cv_access is False` and `cv_action_enabled is False` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py:510-511`) because the patched `cv_get_parent_object` fails. The rewrite must expect the unpatched provider-none page, which is `True`/`True` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py:476-477`), not a softened copy of the old denial.

**Suggestions**
- In the C6 row, write the expected booleans (`True`, `True` for that fixture) so the executor cannot “fix” the test by keeping the denial.

**Risk Assessment:** LOW. The two paths and the named expectation edits are grounded in the current call sites.

## 06.48-03

These tests pin the function rather than a page, which is the right split after the tracer. The permission string `app.change_publisher` matches the existing provider-call shape at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:299` (`app.change_book`). Fixture aliasing matches `tests/test1/test_cancel_permissions.py`.

**Strengths**
- Create target for the nearest-versus-higher key is necessary: the default Read pair cannot tell them apart.
- L2’s three answers are pinned on `helpers.core_book.Create`, and the “allowed” case is tied to the frozen matrix cell instead of being “fixed” in the seam.
- Case (d) matches the request: a missing higher key is `_permission_name` (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:454-460`), which the request does not reach for an actor already refused.

**Concerns**
- LOW: `test_higher_ancestor_gates_order_and_absent_levels` calls `_higher_ancestor_gates` directly. A later edit that gates the same levels inside `_link_permission` but skips the helper would still pass. One two-level call through `_link_access` should sit beside it.

**Suggestions**
- Add one `_link_access` assertion that the Book gate is not called when the Publisher grant is missing, using the same spy as the direct helper test.

**Risk Assessment:** LOW. The contract tests match the decisions and the current building blocks.

## 06.48-04

The two remaining parent-in-hand callers are exactly `ChildContextButton.get_context` (`src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:238-242`) and the child branch of `cv_get_card_action_data` (`src/crud_views/lib/views/card.py:63-64`). The three direct `cv_has_access(..., parent)` lines cited are real (`tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:335`, `:407`, `:1100`). Deleting the adapter only after those callers move is the right order.

**Strengths**
- Passing `(context.object,)` / `(obj,)` and no rendering view keeps today’s `view=None` provider call.
- The private-surface table is the maintainability record the phase needs: one adapter replaced, two state adapters left because cancel and bulk still call them (`src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:82-88`).
- Queryset-only still denies anonymous before own access (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:300-304`); the seam keeps that order and documents the `None` argument change.

**Concerns**
- LOW: `test_query_plain_child_link_cost` line 1100 is inside a query measurement. The plan says restore the line if a number moves. That is right; the executor must not “fix” the baseline by leaving the parent argument in place.

**Suggestions**
- None beyond executing the listed C1/C2 rows as written.

**Risk Assessment:** LOW. Scope is the two sites that already hold the parent, and the adapter cannot disappear before they move.

## 06.48-05

D-31 is implemented as a seam whose core default is `None`, which is the only way to keep core sibling links off the parent gate without a Guardian hook name in core. Today those sites pass the page object (`src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:114`, `src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:913`). `_resolved_ancestor` is the no-query lookup the plan needs (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:576-585`). The URL-parent-versus-cached-FK case is the one that can actually fail a wrong implementation.

**Strengths**
- Empty tuple for “not an own child”, M2M, and no resolved parent matches `_cv_guardian_create_parent` (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:296-297`) without walking the FK.
- Core create stays on `CreateView._cv_action_enabled_for_rendering`, whose unguarded fallback is the existing lookup (`src/crud_views/lib/views/create.py:66-72`), so a hand-built core control does not disappear.
- C9 evidence (old assertion, new assertion, SQL text) is required at the source, not only in plan 09.

**Concerns**
- LOW: List-page create still goes through `ContextButton` first. With `obj is None` the seam returns an empty tuple and D-16 denies before `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` overwrites the dict. That keeps one gate only if the empty-tuple path makes no provider call. The plan says that; the new tests should assert the pre-overwrite call count, not only the final ctx.

**Suggestions**
- In `test_context_controls_send_nested_guardian_create_through_the_check`, add the list page as a negative: seam tuple empty, provider not called until the mixin overwrite.

**Risk Assessment:** LOW. The split between “tuple means the check” and “None means narrowed old path” matches D-31 and the current button code.

## 06.48-06

The four remaining sites pass a parent or a page object today (`src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:184-189` and `:297-301`, `src/crud_views/lib/views/card.py:86-87`, `src/crud_views/templatetags/crud_views.py:651-655`). The filter’s `try`/`except` wraps only the class lookup, and case 4 already calls `_immediate_parent_access` (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:385`), so a malformed policy already escapes the filter. L1 on the grandchild list page is correctly not described as one gate.

**Strengths**
- Detail-page parent button versus list-page overwrite is split into two blocks, with opposite grants, which is what `cv_get_context` actually does.
- Sibling create stays on `cv_has_access(user, None)` today (`src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:301`), which is already `False` for a nested Guardian create (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:382-383`). Handing the check no parent preserves that without calling `cv_create_has_access`.
- The AC-4 test requires 200 and separates enforcement calls from later rendering calls. That matches `cv_get_action_object` (`src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:568-575`).

**Concerns**
- LOW: A D-33 denial stamps `cv_action_enabled=True` (“not evaluated”) where today’s sibling/create path can stamp `False` via `CreateView._cv_action_enabled_for_context` on `None` (`src/crud_views/lib/views/create.py:42-44`). `cv_resolve_action` still yields `disabled:permission` (`src/crud_views/lib/view/visibility.py:19`), so the matrix will not see it. `test_nested_guardian_create_filter_and_sibling_button_stay_denied` should assert the stamp, or a later raw-ctx test will look like a regression the classification rule forbids editing.

**Suggestions**
- Assert `cv_access is False` and `cv_action_enabled is True` on the D-33 sibling button, and no call of either hook.

**Risk Assessment:** LOW. The four sites and L1 are accurately separated; the stamp gap is visible only to raw ctx assertions.

## 06.48-07

Narrowing the hook only after plans 02–06 stop passing a parent is the correct order. The D-25 lines cited exist, including the loop at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:198-208` and the fail-closed companions at `tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py:438` and `:923`. Line 260’s change from `True` to `False` is the provider-none case inside `CaptureQueriesContext`; both the new `False` and the remaining `cv_create_has_access` call stay query-free because provider `"none"` returns before a lookup (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:681-682`).

**Strengths**
- Fail-closed for every argument is the only reading that satisfies D-11, D-15, and the issue constraint together.
- Companions are required to pass on the unchanged hook first, so an inactive or missing-relation rule is not dropped just because `cv_has_access` becomes `False` for everyone.
- The call-site test wraps every registered class of the nested, parent, and sibling ViewSets. `ViewSet.get_all_views` is `self._views` (`src/crud_views/lib/viewset/__init__.py:237-238`), so delete is included if the wrapper iterates the registry rather than a key list.

**Concerns**
- LOW: The line-438 companion sits inside a two-user loop. Adding it once after the loop would miss the superuser. The plan’s acceptance text should say “inside the loop”.

**Suggestions**
- State that the new `cv_create_has_access(..., Book(title="no parent"))` assertion is inside the `for user` loop.

**Risk Assessment:** LOW. This is the public-hook cut, and the test rewrites match the file as it is.

## 06.48-08

The facts match the code the earlier plans leave behind. F11 is the list-page condition at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:332-342`, not a new behavior. The card passages named by line are still the old sentences (`docs/reference/card-list-view.md:296-298`, `:308-312`, `:319`, `:327`, `:329`). The FAQ contradiction is correctly left to plan 09, but it is still present at `docs/faq.md:142-158`.

**Strengths**
- Rewrite-not-append is the right rule for the “parent instance works as before” bullets.
- F4 now includes the queryset-only override, which really receives the parent today (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:304`) and `None` after plan 04.
- The doc test pins site names, `key_target`, and the two extra override cases instead of a vague “migration note exists”.

**Concerns**
- LOW: The Breaking entry can still be read as “every listed parent button is decided by its target’s hook” unless the list-page exception is in the entry itself, not only behind the migration link. The plan says “a few words”; the doc test should require `key_target` in `CHANGELOG.md`, not only in the migration section.

**Suggestions**
- Pin `key_target` in the changelog entry as well as in `## Link access hooks`.

**Risk Assessment:** LOW. The document set matches the decisions, provided F11 is not softened into “the target’s hook always decides”.

## 06.48-09

The gate commit plus a post-gate diff limited to `.planning/` is the right way to stop the record from being measured on a later tree. The one cost change is real: own denial returns before `_immediate_parent_access` today (`src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:530-532`), and the navbar test at `tests/test1/test_navbar_permissions.py:204-205` is the before-value for the child item. The example interpreter path in the verify command exists.

**Strengths**
- Navbar text is limited to the child item; owner items stay on `cv_has_access` (`src/crud_views/lib/navbar.py:84-85`).
- C9 rows are accepted only with SQL text checked against the diff, so a mislabeled drop cannot become an “intended” cost change.
- L1 and L2 are carried into the summary as maintainer decisions, with the cells and tests that pin them.

**Concerns**
- LOW: The FAQ rewrite is accurate, but `test_reference_pages_state_the_narrowed_meaning` should also fail if `docs/faq.md:149-150` still says the filter checks the page object’s parent. Naming `cv_create_has_access` once is not enough if the old sentence remains.

**Suggestions**
- Pin the absence of “keep passing the page's own object” in that FAQ entry.

**Risk Assessment:** LOW. This plan measures the phase claims instead of restating them.

## Cross-plan

The nine plans do the stage-1 job: one private check, parent-in-hand sites moved, sibling-page sites narrowed with only a nested Guardian create taking the check, then `cv_has_access` fail-closed, then the Breaking entry. Call-site inventory matches `src/`. Wave order is safe: the hook is not narrowed until package callers stop passing a parent. D-14 is an empty diff of `ancestors.py` and `ancestor_db.py`, which the plans never edit.

The main hole is the inactive Guardian page slice of plan 01. Later plans treat that matrix as proof of an unchanged rendered result. For those pages the request gate never renders the control (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:56-58`), so a link-check change for inactive would not fail a cell. Fix that capture before plan 02, or the rest of the phase is proving a weaker statement than issue #407.

Overall risk: MEDIUM, only because of that baseline gap. The production design itself is bounded, matches the current adapters, and does not need another abstraction.

## OpenCode Review (gemini-review)

## 06.48-01

### 1. Summary
Plan `06.48-01` establishes the Wave 0 characterization baseline on the unchanged tree (`dbf1e6c4`) before any production code is modified. It captures 1,178 uniquely identified cells across parent-in-hand controls (`ChildContextButton`, child `CardAction`, object navbar child `NavItem`), special scenarios (`plain-3` farther ancestor under D-26, `guardian-queryset-only` under D-30, `plain-resource` leaf, and `core-guardian` under known limit L2), and sibling-page controls (`create-control`, `home-button`, `parent-button` including `guardian-3` for known limit L1, `sibling-button`, `row-action`, `card-action`, and the `cv_context_has_permission` filter) into `tests/test1/link_access_matrix_baseline.json`, and records the cost baseline in `06.48-COST-BASELINE.md`.

### 2. Strengths
- **True characterization baseline:** Capturing normalized outcomes (`hidden`, `enabled`, `disabled:permission`, `disabled:state`, `page:<status>`) on `dbf1e6c4` into a static JSON file and removing the writer path before committing ensures `test_link_matrix_cell` never computes expected outcomes from the code under test.
- **Complete Round 1 coverage:** All Round 1 findings against this plan (A-01 through A-07, A-12, and the matrix portion of H-01) are cleanly resolved: seven-segment case IDs (`control/composition/page/target/actor/state/mode`) prevent key collisions, exact cell counts (544 after Task 01, 1,178 after Task 02) are enforced in `verify`, inactive client sessions use `INACTIVE_BACKENDS` with `force_login(actor, backend=ALLOW_INACTIVE)` (`tests/test1/test_authorization_matrix.py:28-40`), and both pre-existing limits L1 (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:328-356`) and L2 (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:339-344`) have dedicated discriminating cells.
- **Explicit before-values for unmeasured own-denied paths:** Task 03 explicitly records the pre-change behavior of `test_query_denied_actors_and_custom_do_no_permission_work` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1254-1269`) and `test_reached_policy_errors_propagate` (`tests/test1/test_navbar_permissions.py:167-206`) so the intentional C2 step-order change in Plans 02 and 04 has a documented baseline.

### 3. Concerns
- **LOW — `card-action` cells on `CardListView` cannot be captured via `cv_get_context` (`_record_contexts`):** Task 02 instructs the executor to "Record the ctx dict the page built during the request (the `_record_contexts` pattern: a spy on the page class's `cv_get_context`...)" for the controls in Task 02, which include `card-action` (targets `detail` and `create` on a child `card` page). However, `CardListView.cv_get_card_action_data` (`src/crud_views/lib/views/card.py:76-91`) builds its dict via `cls.cv_get_dict(...)` and never calls `self.cv_get_context`. Worse, `CardListView` also has `cv_context_actions = crud_views_settings.list_context_actions` (`src/crud_views/lib/views/card.py:33`, `PnBookCardView.cv_context_actions = ["create"]` at `tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py:136`), which *does* call `cv_get_context(key="create", obj=None)`. Spying on `cv_get_context` on the card page would capture the top-toolbar `create` context button instead of the row `CardAction(key="create")` and would find no record at all for `CardAction(key="detail")`.
- **LOW — Missing views and context actions on reused fixtures for `create-control` and `filter`:** Task 02 reuses `pn` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py:100-260`) and the shipped test-app views (`tests/test1/app/views.py:475-481`). In `pn`, `PnBookListView` (`:118`) has `cv_context_actions = ["parent", "create"]` (no `"create_alias"`), `PnBookCreateView` (`:140`) inherits `create_context_actions = ["home", "create"]` (`src/crud_views/lib/settings.py:117`, no `"create_alias"`), and `pn.genre_vs` (`:150-169`) only registers `list` and `create` views (no `detail` or `update` view and no `create_alias` button). Without explicitly adding `"create_alias"` to the `list` and `create` pages and registering `detail`/`update` views on the M2M `Genre` ViewSet, those cells of the 336 `create-control` and 84 `filter` matrices will not be recorded.
- **LOW — Default two-item `NavBar` in `permission_routes` when checking `navbar-child` presence:** In `tests/test1/test_navbar_permissions.py:43`, `permission_routes` constructs `bar = NavBar(viewset=owner.name, items=[NavItem(key="detail", label="Details"), NavItem(child=child.name, key=key)])`. For an actor like `direct-view` with `target="create"`, the owner `detail` item is allowed while the child `create` item is denied, so `data["cv_navbar"]` is non-empty (`len(items) == 1`). Checking `bool(data["cv_navbar"])` instead of restricting `ns.bar.items = [NavItem(child=ns.child.name, key=key)]` (as `test_actor_and_provider_controls` does at `:215`) would misrecord the child item as `enabled`.

### 4. Suggestions
- In Task 02, include `tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py` lines 930–970 in `read_first` and specify that `card-action` cells record the dict returned by `cv_get_card_action_data` (the `_record_card_actions` spy at `:931-941`), not `cv_get_context`.
- In Task 02, note that `"create_alias"` must be present in `cv_context_actions` on the `list` and `create` pages as well as `detail`/`update`/`delete`, and that the `guardian-m2m` composition needs `detail` and `update` views registered alongside `list` and `create`.
- In Task 01, specify setting `ns.bar.items = [NavItem(child=ns.child.name, key=key)]` (or matching the child item in `cv_navbar["items"]`) when evaluating `navbar-child` cells.

### 5. Risk Assessment
**LOW** — This plan modifies no production files (`src/`, `docs/`, `CHANGELOG.md`, and `tests/test1/golden/` are verified unchanged against `dbf1e6c4`), and the cell-count gates (544 and 1,178) immediately catch any missing or collided cell IDs.

## 06.48-02

### 1. Summary
Plan `06.48-02` is the tracer wave: it introduces `src/crud_views/lib/link_access.py` (`_link_permission`, `_link_access`, `_link_ctx`, `_higher_ancestor_gates`, and the three outcome constants), adds the private classmethod seam `_cv_link_permission` on `CrudView`, `GuardianQuerysetMixin`, and `GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired`, and moves two representative call sites (`navbar._build` for child items at `src/crud_views/lib/navbar.py:84-88` and `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:328-357`) onto the check while updating the affected navbar and Guardian list-page tests.

### 2. Strengths
- **Zero-diff guarantee on request-gate modules (D-14):** Placing `_link_access` in `src/crud_views/lib/link_access.py` and reusing the existing helpers in `src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py` and `src/crud_views/lib/ancestor_db.py` leaves both gate modules byte-identical to `dbf1e6c4`.
- **Clean per-target seam (`_cv_link_permission`):** Each of the three classes (`CrudView`, `GuardianQuerysetMixin`, `GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired`) keeps the exact rule it owns today (core actor rule after nearest gate, Guardian stricter actor rule and queryset-only exception under D-30, and `cv_create_has_access` only under D-32) without leaking Guardian hook names into core.
- **Thorough resolution of Round 1 findings (H-01, A-07, A-09, A-10, A-11):**
  - The 5-step sequence of `_higher_ancestor_gates` matches `_authorize_ancestors` (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:464-567`), returning `True` before the actor rule when no higher level is gated and skipping `None` elements above the nearest level (D-16).
  - Known limits L1 (`GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` matching `key_target` across button subclasses) and L2 (`GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired._cv_link_permission` delegating actor checks to `cv_create_has_access`) are explicitly preserved so no rendered result changes in stage 1.
  - The per-spy expectations in `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:150-152` are explicitly unrolled to account for `(current_actor, None, resolved_owner, rendering_view=None)` on `_cv_link_permission` and `(current_actor, resolved_owner)` on `cv_action_enabled`.

### 3. Concerns
- **LOW — Transient double state-hook call on permitted Guardian list-page `create` between Plan 02 and Plan 05:** In Plan 02, `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:326`) still calls `super().cv_get_context(...)` before running `_link_access(target_cls, user, None, known, rendering_view=self)`. Until Plan 05 updates `CrudView._cv_get_action_context` and `ContextButton.get_context`, `super().cv_get_context` runs `GuardianBookCreateView._cv_action_enabled_for_rendering(user, None, self)` (`src/crud_views/lib/views/create.py:52-75`), which calls `self.cv_get_parent_object()` and invokes `cv_action_enabled(user, parent_obj)` once before `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` overwrites `ctx`. As noted in Plan 02's table row for `test_list_create_control_late_parent_failure_skips_state_hook` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py:495-514`), existing list-page tests only check `all(isinstance(obj, Publisher) for obj in pn.hook_calls)` and `None not in pn.hook_calls` (not call count or empty `hook_calls` on permission denial for `BookList`), and Plan 05 eliminates the base call by routing `GuardianBookCreateView` through `_cv_page_link_ancestors` -> `()`.

### 4. Suggestions
- In Task 03's note on `test_list_create_control_late_parent_failure_skips_state_hook`, keep the note that `super().cv_get_context` still invokes the state adapter once on the child list page until Plan 05 introduces `_cv_page_link_ancestors`, so the executor understands why `pn.hook_calls` is still populated during `super().cv_get_context` in Wave 2 and why `None not in pn.hook_calls` remains the right assertion.

### 5. Risk Assessment
**LOW** — The tracer touches only two call sites, preserves `view=None` on navbar child items and `view=self` on the Guardian list page so query totals and access-trace owners cannot shift, and verifies against the 1,178-cell matrix and full test suite.

## 06.48-07

### 1. Summary
Plan `06.48-07` narrows `GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired.cv_has_access` (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:347-385`) so that a nested Guardian create returns `False` for every `obj` argument (removing cases 3 and 4), updates the docstrings of `GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired`, `cv_create_has_access`, `cv_has_access`, and `CrudView.cv_action_enabled`, rewrites the direct-call helper assertions in `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py` (D-25) and the four Guardian test files (`test_guardian.py`, `test_guardian_parent_none.py`, `test_guardian_inactive.py`, `test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py`), and adds `test_nested_guardian_create_cv_has_access_fails_closed_for_every_argument` and `test_no_package_call_site_passes_a_parent_to_cv_has_access`.

- **Fail-closed narrowing of `cv_has_access` (D-11, D-25):** Having `GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired.cv_has_access` return `False` for every `obj` when nested eliminates the dual meaning of `obj` before the 1.0 freeze and fails closed for any direct caller still passing a parent or an own child.
- **Comprehensive dynamic call-site verification (Round 1 A-14):** `test_no_package_call_site_passes_a_parent_to_cv_has_access` wraps `cv_has_access` across all registered view classes of the nested, parent, and sibling ViewSets in both core and Guardian compositions, verifying both that every control using `cv_has_access` is actually observed (with `None` or an own-model instance) and that no control linking to a nested Guardian create calls `cv_has_access` at all (D-32).

### 3. Concerns
- **LOW — Two un-paired denial assertions in `tests/test1/test_guardian.py` could be named explicitly in the "A denial that keeps passing" table:** In `tests/test1/test_guardian.py:386-411` (`test_create_cv_has_access_parent_none_policy_resolves_without_permission`), line 399 (`assert GuardianBookCreateView.cv_has_access(user_guardian, publisher_a) is False`, the strict-policy control for `publisher_a`) and line 410 (`assert GuardianBookCreateView.cv_has_access(user_guardian, author_douglas_adams) is False`, the unrelated-object denial under `provider="none"`) have no `cv_create_has_access(user_guardian, None, ...)` companion in that test (line 400 checks `book_under_publisher_a`, not `publisher_a`, and line 409 checks `None`, not `author_douglas_adams`). Similarly, `test_create_cv_has_access_child_with_parent_obj_without_perm` (`tests/test1/test_guardian.py:327-332`) is listed in the file table to be updated, where `cv_has_access(user_guardian, publisher_a)` already returned `False`. While the plan instructs Task 01 to check the four files once more and apply the companion rule, naming lines 331, 399, and 410 of `tests/test1/test_guardian.py` explicitly in the table alongside `tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py:438, 923` removes any guesswork.

### 4. Suggestions
- Add `tests/test1/test_guardian.py:331` (`test_create_cv_has_access_child_with_parent_obj_without_perm`), `:399`, and `:410` (`test_create_cv_has_access_parent_none_policy_resolves_without_permission`) to the table in "A denial that keeps passing" so all un-paired denials across the four files are explicitly enumerated.

## Consensus Summary

Cycle 2. Three reviewers produced a review with `file:line` evidence, and each covered all nine plans (the plan
coverage check finds every plan id in all three reviews). None of the three sections opens with an
evidence-quality discount marker, so all three count at full weight. grok-review and gemini-review share the
OpenCode adapter: where they agree, the agreement is cross-model, not cross-tool.

No reviewer disputes a locked decision. astra-review states that "D-26 and D-30 are accepted stage-1 limits, not
findings". Two astra concerns are about the known limits that `06.48-CONTEXT.md:130-148` records as awaiting the
maintainer's confirmation: L1 (H2-01 below) and the narrowing of D-13 (listed under "already covered"). Neither
names a new defect in how the plans handle the limit. No reviewer raises L2 as a finding. gemini-review, which
raised H-01 in cycle 1, writes: "Every HIGH, MEDIUM, and LOW concern from Round 1 (`H-01`, `A-01` through
`A-24`, `FD-1`) has been thoroughly addressed in the revised plans".

Counts for this cycle: one HIGH concern, eight actionable non-HIGH concerns. The ledger below lists each one.

### Agreed Strengths

- **Baseline before any production edit.** The matrix is captured on the unchanged tree and the committed test only
  reads the frozen file, so "same rendered result" is checkable. The revision closed the cycle 1 holes: seven-segment
  ids, exact cell counts in the verify step, cells that tell known limit L1 apart
  (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:328-356`). (astra, grok, gemini)
- **Gate modules stay untouched, and each site keeps today's `view=` value.** D-14 is an empty diff of
  `ancestors.py` and `ancestor_db.py`. The checker and the trace owner do not move
  (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:61`, `:94`). (astra, grok, gemini)
- **The per-target seams.** `_cv_link_permission` keeps each of the three rules in the class that owns it, and a
  nested Guardian create answers with `cv_create_has_access` alone, without a second gate
  (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:339`). `_cv_page_link_ancestors` with a core default of `None` keeps core
  sibling links off the parent gate without a Guardian name in core. (astra, grok, gemini)
- **`_higher_ancestor_gates` follows the request's order** (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:464-567`): static pair,
  actor admission, then per-level work; "no level to gate" answers before the actor rule. (grok, gemini)
- **The unit tests test the hard parts.** A create target whose Write pair has different nearest and higher keys
  proves the key selection (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:397`). The case "URL parent A, cached foreign key B"
  is the one that fails a kept foreign-key path (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:298`). (astra, grok, gemini)
- **Core create controls keep their adapter**, so a hand-built core control does not disappear
  (`src/crud_views/lib/views/create.py:63-72`). (astra, grok, gemini)
- **Link and request are compared with the two kinds of call kept apart.** Enforcement runs before the page is
  built (`src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:1321`), and the test takes the request side from the calls before
  `get_context_data`. (astra, grok, gemini)
- **Order of the waves.** `cv_has_access` on a nested Guardian create is narrowed only after every package caller
  has moved off the parent cases. The D-25 line tables match `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py`. (astra, grok,
  gemini)
- **The one changed cost is real and named.** Own denial returns before the parent gate today
  (`src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:530`), and `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1265` pins zero
  queries. (astra, grok)
- **Documentation is rewritten, not appended to.** `docs/migrating-1.0.md:1885` tells callers today that a parent
  argument still works. F4 names the queryset-only override, and the card passages are named by line. (astra,
  grok, gemini)

### Agreed Concerns

No concern was raised by two reviewers with the same case. One theme was raised by all three, each with a
different case, and is the highest priority of this cycle:

1. **A matrix cell of plan 01 can be counted without recording its control.** astra: a cell that raises is frozen
   as `raises:<class>` and counts toward the total (A2-01). grok: every page-level cell of the `inactive` and
   `anonymous` actors records the refused page, not the control (A2-02). gemini: the `card-action` cells are
   captured through a spy on `cv_get_context`, which a card action never calls, so the spy sees the toolbar's
   create control instead (A2-03). The exact cell counts do not catch any of the three, because each case yields a
   cell with a valid id.

A second theme was raised by two reviewers, again with different cases:

2. **The table "A denial that keeps passing" of plan 07.** gemini: two unpaired denials in
   `tests/test1/test_guardian.py` are not named, and the plan says the denials of that file are already paired
   (A2-07). grok: the companion for line 438 of `tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py` must sit inside the loop
   (covered by the plan's wording, see below).

### Divergent Views

- **Whether known limit L1 blocks execution.** astra rates it HIGH for readiness, because the maintainer's
  confirmation is pending (H2-01). grok and gemini treat the handling as correct: grok names the split between
  the list page and the detail page as a strength of plan 06, and gemini, which raised H-01 in cycle 1, calls it
  resolved. No reviewer asks for a different implementation.
- **The recording test of plan 07.** gemini calls it "comprehensive dynamic call-site verification", and grok
  confirms that iterating the registry includes the delete class
  (`src/crud_views/lib/viewset/__init__.py:237-238`). astra shows that two controls with one target class satisfy
  each other's row (A2-06).
- **The report-only check of plan 09.** grok and gemini name it as a strength. astra shows that it covers five
  paths, not everything outside `.planning/` (A2-08). grok describes the check as "a post-gate diff limited to
  `.planning/`", which is what the truth says and not what the command does (`06.48-09-PLAN.md:229`).
- **The `None` state object on an unguarded view.** astra rates the open wording of D-13 MEDIUM. gemini counts
  FD-1 of cycle 1 as addressed. grok does not raise it.
- **Risk per plan.** astra: plan 02 HIGH "for readiness", plans 01, 05, 07 and 09 MEDIUM, plans 03, 04, 06 and 08
  LOW. grok: plan 01 MEDIUM, all others LOW, overall MEDIUM "only because of that baseline gap". gemini: every
  plan LOW, overall LOW.

### Concern ledger — cycle 2

Severity is the reviewer's. "Checked" says what the orchestrator confirmed in source or in the plan text. The IDs
carry the cycle (`H2-`, `A2-`) so that they cannot be confused with the cycle 1 IDs the plans' ledgers cite.

Case: a `ParentContextButton(key_target="create")` on a grandchild Guardian list page; the parent ViewSet's create hook denies, the page's own create allows, and `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:328` overwrites the button's denial. astra: "Preservation is defensible, but it does not satisfy an unconditional promise that the destination hook governs every shown control." - gemini, plan 01, LOW: `create_alias` is not in the context actions of the `pn` list and create pages, and the
  `ManyToMany` Genre ViewSet of `pn` registers no detail view. The plan defines the control and its pages
  (`06.48-01-PLAN.md:244`), allows an isolated ViewSet where no fixture provides the page (`06.48-01-PLAN.md:253`),
  and a missing cell fails the exact count (`06.48-01-PLAN.md:265`, `:279`). Naming the two gaps is a suggestion.
- gemini, plan 01, LOW: `permission_routes` builds a navbar with an owner item and a child item
  (`tests/test1/test_navbar_permissions.py:43`), so testing the navbar for emptiness would record a denied child
  item as `enabled`. The plan defines the outcome by the item: "`enabled` when the item is present in the built
  navbar data" (`06.48-01-PLAN.md:179`). Naming the two-item bar is a suggestion.
- gemini, plan 02, LOW: between plan 02 and plan 05 the base path still calls the state adapter once on the child
  list page. The plan says so (`06.48-02-PLAN.md:268`, "Until plan 05 the unchanged base path still calls the
  patched method once ... the test does not assert a call count"). The reviewer asks to keep the note.

## Historical review — cycle 1

The following is the preserved cycle 1 body, unchanged: reviewed revision `ea580a08`, report commit `0a91f67f`.
Its concerns are historical. The plans rule on each of them in their `## Review Dispositions Ledger`, under
`### Round 1 — 0a91f67f`, and cite the lines of that commit (`L###@0a91f67f`); `git show 0a91f67f:<this file>` gives
the file those line numbers refer to. Current dispositions are in the cycle 2 sections above. Nothing below this
heading counts as a current finding.

Cycle 1 reviews the committed plans at `ea580a08`. The three configured reviewers
(`review.default_reviewers`) were dispatched together (`review.parallel_lanes: true`), each with the same
unshortened prompt. astra-review and grok-review returned on the first attempt. The first gemini-review attempt was
killed by the lane's 1200-second timeout before it wrote a final message (stop reason `tool-calls`). It was run
again once, alone, with the same prompt and the same timeout, and returned a full review. The section below is the
second attempt; the stub of the first is kept with the diagnostics. All three reviews cover all nine plans.

Diagnostics: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/.review-diagnostics/20261123T151034Z-gsd-review-naKdl2`. No plan, state file or product code was changed by
this review.

## Codex Review (astra-review)

Reviewed against checkout `ea580a08`; `src/`, `tests/`, `docs/`, and `CHANGELOG.md` match `dbf1e6c4`. A focused baseline run passed **176 tests** on Python 3.12.3 / Django 6.0.10. The proposed implementation does not exist yet; findings distinguish verified source behavior from predicted implementation behavior.

## 06.48-01

**Summary:** Capturing outcomes before production edits is a strong foundation, but the snapshot specification needs correction before capture.

**Strengths**

- Freezing resolved visibility outcomes matches the actual renderer: permission denial takes precedence over state denial in `src/crud_views/lib/view/visibility.py:4`.
- The cost baselines measure meaningful behavior. `tests/test1/test_ancestor_costs.py:172` rejects duplicate gates, and `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:157` pins query totals.

**Concerns**

- **MEDIUM — Snapshot IDs collide.** Task 02 uses `control/composition/page/actor/state`, but assigns multiple targets to the same control: create/update filters, list/create sibling buttons, detail/create card actions, and direct/aliased create controls. Those cases cannot occupy distinct JSON keys. Set equality does not detect duplicate generated IDs. Evidence: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md:172` and `:182`. These are genuinely different calls: the filter selects its destination by `key` in `src/crud_views/templatetags/crud_views.py:652`.
- **MEDIUM — Two promised scenarios lack capture instructions.** The must-haves require farther-ancestor denial and queryset-only composition cells, but the enumerated dimensions do not include either. Existing tests construct these explicitly at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:500` and `:767`; ordinary Guardian composition does not exercise the queryset-only exception.

**Suggestions**

- Include destination and entry-point variant in every case ID. Assert `len(case_ids) == len(set(case_ids))` before constructing the snapshot dictionary.
- Add explicit scenario rows for both accepted exceptions, including their refused-click evidence.

**Risk Assessment: MEDIUM.** Without these fixes, the central preservation artifact cannot establish its claimed coverage.

## 06.48-02

**Summary:** The permission seam is well grounded in existing implementation differences. One protected-actor path does not satisfy the promised contract.

**Strengths**

- Avoiding a separate generic parent gate before `cv_create_has_access` prevents duplicate work: the existing hook already calls `_immediate_parent_access` at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:344`.
- Preserving each site’s rendering-view argument is justified. `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:66` creates a fresh checker for `None`, while `:68` reuses a view-local checker; `:94` also changes trace ownership.

**Concerns**

- **MEDIUM — Nested Guardian create bypasses the protected-actor rule under a core parent.** The proposed override returns `cv_create_has_access` directly and “calls nothing else”: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md:270`.
  
  Concrete input: an anonymous actor, a protected core parent, a nested Guardian create, and a backend granting the parent permission. `_guardian_inactive_actor` explicitly excludes anonymous users (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:35`), while the core provider simply returns `user.has_perm` (`src/crud_views/lib/viewset/__init__.py:509`). I reproduced `cv_create_has_access(...) == True` with these inputs.
  
  With one supplied ancestor, the proposed check reaches that answer without an authenticated-and-active check. Expected under D-18: permission denial without evaluating state. The request remains protected by ancestor actor admission at `src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:494`; this is a link-contract defect, not demonstrated request authorization bypass.

**Suggestions**

- Explicitly enforce D-18 for this path without adding a second parent gate or changing the request gate.
- Add the core-parent/Guardian-create composition with a permissive anonymous backend to the tests.

**Risk Assessment: MEDIUM.** Resolve the actor-rule gap before treating the private check as proven.

## 06.48-03

**Summary:** The unit-test categories are appropriate, particularly ancestor ordering, missing-parent denial, and direct-hook safety. Actor coverage needs a more explicit composition matrix.

**Strengths**

- Testing an unprotected immediate parent with a protected higher ancestor exercises real policy behavior: `_selected_gates` assigns `"nearest"` to the first protected level at `src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:399`.
- Ordered provider-call assertions can detect more than final-result tests. The existing spy records ViewSet, permission, and object at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:140`.

**Concerns**

- **MEDIUM — The actor test can miss the Guardian-create path.** Its specification expressly names core targets and Guardian lists, but does not require a nested Guardian create under a core provider: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-03-PLAN.md:143`. That composition already exists in the suggested fixture at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:69`, so this omission is inexpensive to close.

**Suggestions**

- Parameterize protected-actor checks across core targets, Guardian lists, and Guardian creates, with both model and Guardian parent providers where applicable.
- Require the negative cases to prove that the state hook was not called.
- Add a `cascade=False` case to complement the explicitly enabled cascade case.

**Risk Assessment: MEDIUM.** The tests otherwise cover the function’s essential contract well.

## 06.48-04

**Summary:** This is a bounded consumer migration with a credible adapter-removal argument. I found no additional blocking defect.

**Strengths**

- Both migrated consumers already possess the target’s immediate parent: `ChildContextButton` uses `context.object` at `src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:229`, and child card actions use the row at `src/crud_views/lib/views/card.py:58`.
- The named cost change is real. The current adapter returns on own-access denial before reaching the parent gate at `src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:530`.
- Keeping the two state adapters is justified by their remaining delegation chain at `src/crud_views/lib/views/create.py:75`.

**Concerns**

- No additional source-backed blocker found. The principal execution risk is weakening negative tests while changing their spies; the plan’s explicit rewrite table substantially limits that risk.

**Suggestions**

- Preserve the explicit parent-identity assertions when moving spies.
- Retain separate assertions for anonymous/inactive short-circuiting and custom own-access denial; those currently share one loop at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1262`, but their expected costs diverge.

**Risk Assessment: LOW**, assuming the private check’s actor handling is corrected.

## 06.48-05

**Summary:** The `None` versus tuple dispatch contract preserves the stage boundary and uses the existing resolved-ancestor mechanism. Its tests should more directly distinguish URL-parent selection from cached foreign-key selection.

**Strengths**

- `_resolved_ancestor` reads existing guard state without fetching rows at `src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:579`.
- Keeping core create adapters preserves an existing supported fallback: `src/crud_views/lib/views/create.py:63` resolves the URL parent, and the tests deliberately exercise hand-built views at `tests/test1/test_core_nested_create_state.py:185`.

**Concerns**

- **MEDIUM — D-27’s critical distinction is insufficiently pinned.** The proposed positive test supplies an own child and its resolved parent, but does not explicitly make that parent differ from the child’s cached FK: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-05-PLAN.md:251`.
  
  An implementation accidentally retaining the old FK path at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:299` could pass an ordinary matching-parent test and a zero-query assertion when the relation is cached.

**Suggestions**

- Add a focused case with resolved URL parent A and an own-child input whose cached FK points to B.
- Assert that the create hook receives A, B is never consulted, and no relation lookup occurs. The existing core test at `tests/test1/test_core_nested_create_state.py:178` provides a useful pattern.

**Risk Assessment: MEDIUM**, primarily a verification gap rather than a defect in the prescribed implementation.

## 06.48-06

**Summary:** The remaining consumer changes are appropriately narrow. Permission-only filtering and real-request state-object comparisons are particularly useful.

**Strengths**

- Keeping the filter permission-only preserves its existing behavior at `src/crud_views/templatetags/crud_views.py:650`.
- Retaining the parent button’s object lookup is justified: it supplies an object destination as well as collection destinations at `src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:162` and `:179`.
- The link/request comparison targets the actual request-side rule in `src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:571`, including nested lists.

**Concerns**

- No additional blocking defect found.
- **LOW — Ensure comparison tests cannot pass vacuously.** The prescribed comparison records state arguments during full requests: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-06-PLAN.md:275`. Requests can evaluate several controls, so comparing undifferentiated call collections could attribute a rendering call to request enforcement.

**Suggestions**

- Assert successful responses and nonempty recordings for both sides.
- Isolate the destination’s enforcement call from calls caused by rendering its own controls.

**Risk Assessment: LOW.**

## 06.48-07

**Summary:** Narrowing the public hook after migrating consumers is sound. The direct-call rewrite inventory is unusually precise.

**Strengths**

- The proposed nested branch removes precisely the old overloaded behavior at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:385`, while preserving the top-level delegation at `:379`.
- Moving parent-rule assertions to `cv_create_has_access` preserves meaningful permission tests instead of replacing everything with unconditional-denial assertions. The existing paired assertions at `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:260` illustrate why this matters.

**Concerns**

- **LOW — The “every call site” spy omits a listed destination.** The test installs wrappers on list, detail, update, and create, but also renders delete actions: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-07-PLAN.md:233`. Guardian delete is a separate class at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:236`; wrapping the other concrete classes does not observe its calls.

**Suggestions**

- Include delete in the wrapper set.
- Assert expected positive call counts per exercised destination so an accidentally unobserved path cannot satisfy the argument assertions.
- Describe the dynamic test’s coverage separately from the exhaustive source audit.

**Risk Assessment: LOW.**

## 06.48-08

**Summary:** The migration coverage is strong and addresses the important override changes. One existing contradictory statement should be explicitly included in the edit list.

**Strengths**

- Rewriting the Guardian input guidance is necessary: the current hook documents parent and own-child inputs at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:362`.
- Documenting `rendering_view=None` is important because the current public hook documentation advertises access to `rendering_view.request` and `.kwargs` at `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/views.py:331`.

**Concerns**

- **LOW — The action-enabled introduction is omitted from the explicit edits.** It currently promises that state runs after `cv_has_access` has passed at `docs/reference/action_enabled.md:3`. The task specifically edits the object table and enforcement section, but not that opening claim: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-08-PLAN.md:200`.
  
  Leaving it would contradict the retained state-first context-button path at `src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:109`.

**Suggestions**

- Explicitly rewrite the introduction and include it in the documentation pin.
- State that overrides must not assume prior permission approval, regardless of which consumer invokes them.

**Risk Assessment: LOW.** The existing acceptance criteria should catch this, but naming the passage avoids an unnecessary execution failure.

## 06.48-09

**Summary:** The closing checks are comprehensive. The verification record needs a precise definition of the revision being verified.

**Strengths**

- Repeating the existing measurements is stronger than relying on unchanged test names: `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:160` records query categories and hook counts, while `tests/test1/test_ancestor_costs.py:145` records gate events.
- Separately identifying the own-denied cost change preserves the meaning of the old zero-work assertion at `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1268`.

**Concerns**

- **LOW — “Final commit” is ambiguous while creating the verification record.** The task appends the after-record, runs checks “at the final commit,” and subsequently produces a summary: `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-09-PLAN.md:162` and `:168`. A later report commit necessarily changes HEAD, making a literal final-commit claim inaccurate.

**Suggestions**

- Record the exact tested commit and whether the relevant working tree was clean.
- Treat subsequent report-only commits explicitly as such. If executable code, tests, or relevant documentation changes after verification, rerun the affected checks.
- Preserve machine-readable measurement output alongside the comparison, where practical.

**Risk Assessment: LOW.**

## Overall assessment

**Coverage: 9/9 plans. Overall risk: MEDIUM. Recommend targeted revisions before execution.**

The architecture and staging largely achieve the approved scope. The most important fixes are:

1. Make snapshot IDs unique and explicitly capture the two promised exception scenarios.
2. Enforce and test protected-actor admission for nested Guardian creates under core parents.
3. Add an adversarial URL-parent-versus-FK test.
4. Correct the smaller spy, documentation, and verification-provenance gaps.

I did not treat the accepted farther-ancestor or queryset-only visibility exceptions as defects, and found no reason to reopen the settled architecture. No repository files were changed; shipping and codebase mapping were not performed as part of this review.

## 06.48-01

**Summary.** A pre-refactor characterization matrix is the right way to prove "same rendered result." - Cost record is separate from golden files, so `tests/test1/golden/` can stay byte-identical.

**Concerns.**
- MEDIUM: Task 01's acceptance wants 504 cells, but `<verify>` only fails below 300 passed tests. A partial matrix can go green.
- MEDIUM: Navbar admission never builds a ctx dict (`src/crud_views/lib/navbar.py:84-88` skips the item). `mode=disable` and `state=off` cannot produce `disabled:*` for `navbar-child`, so those 84 cells do not pin a reason.
- LOW: No Resource leaf cell. Research pitfall 9 is unaddressed.

**Suggestions.** Make the verify command assert the JSON key count. Record navbar outcomes as `hidden`/`enabled` only, and say so in the completeness test.

## 06.48-02

Keeping `ancestors.py` and `ancestor_db.py` untouched makes D-14 a diff check. Navbar child items cannot be object views (`src/crud_views/lib/navbar.py:66-69`), so passing `obj=None` is correct.

- Inside-function import of `_immediate_parent_access` is visible to `parent_spy` (`tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:615-630`).
- C2 is real: `test_reached_policy_errors_propagate` renders an empty bar for `allowed=False` only because own denial returns first (`base.py:530-531`, test at `test_navbar_permissions.py:204-205`).

- MEDIUM: `test_navbar_costs.py:151` asserts `call_args.args[1] is resolved_owner` for every spy. The new seam's second argument is `None`. The plan says to change "the assertion" but does not cite this shared loop.

## Cross-plan

The wave order is the main safety property. `cv_has_access` on a nested Guardian create still interprets a parent (`views.py:362-385`) until plan 07, and plans 05–06 move every package caller off that meaning first. Reversing 06 and 07 would hide controls the matrix is there to protect.

The private surface (two seams, five module functions, two state adapters kept) is larger than the concept's "one function," and it is the smallest shape that keeps Guardian rules out of core. D-31 forbids the simpler "send every sibling target through the check" option, and the plans follow that.

Overall risk: MEDIUM. The plans achieve the phase goal if the two unlisted override cases are added to the Breaking entry and the navbar cost spy loop is split as specified.

## OpenCode Review (gemini-review)

## 06.48-01

### 1. Summary
`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md` establishes the Wave 1 characterization baseline on the unchanged tree (`dbf1e6c4`) before any production code is edited. It freezes the normalized rendered outcomes (`hidden`, `enabled`, `disabled:permission`, `disabled:state`, or `True`/`False` for the template filter) of both parent-in-hand controls (`ChildContextButton`, child `CardAction`, `NavBar` child item) and sibling-page controls (`create` action/alias, `home`, `parent`, `sibling`, row `detail`, non-child card `detail`/`create`, and `cv_context_has_permission`) into `tests/test1/link_access_matrix_baseline.json`, and records the query and gate-event baseline in `06.48-COST-BASELINE.md`.

### 2. Strengths
- **True characterization separation (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md:123-128`):** The committed `tests/test1/test_link_access_matrix.py` only reads `tests/test1/link_access_matrix_baseline.json` and contains no write/regeneration path, preventing later waves from accidentally regenerating expectations against modified code.
- **Explicit pins for kept `False`/hidden edge cases (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md:153-163`):** `test_link_matrix_snapshot_is_complete` explicitly verifies that the baseline captures the two D-27 `False` filter answers (`guardian` child `list` page and `guardian-m2m` child `detail` page) and the two D-33 denied controls (`create-control` on the `guardian` `create` page and `sibling-button` to the `guardian` `create`).
- **Pre-accounting for unmeasured own-denied child-link work (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md:193-201`):** Task `06.48-01-03` explicitly notes that `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:1254` (`test_query_denied_actors_and_custom_do_no_permission_work`) and `tests/test1/test_navbar_permissions.py:167` (`test_reached_policy_errors_propagate`) do not record a `record_property` measurement on the unchanged tree, establishing the exact before-values for Plan 09's comparison.

### 3. Concerns
- **MEDIUM — `inactive` actor session authentication in Django test client requests (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md:139-151`):**
  - *Input:* Task `06.48-01-02` runs page-level cells through the Django test client (`Client.force_login(actor)`) for the seven actors including `inactive`.
  - *What happens:* Under Django's default `ModelBackend` (`django.contrib.auth.backends.ModelBackend.user_can_authenticate`), `Client.force_login` on a user with `is_active=False` causes `contrib.auth.get_user(request)` to reject the session and downgrade `request.user` to `AnonymousUser()` unless an inactive-permitting backend is configured (`tests/test1/test_authorization_matrix.py:31-32`, `ALLOW_INACTIVE = "django.contrib.auth.backends.AllowAllUsersModelBackend"`).
  - *What should happen:* For the `inactive` actor in Task `06.48-01-02`, the test must override `AUTHENTICATION_BACKENDS` with `INACTIVE_BACKENDS` and log in via `client.force_login(actor, backend=ALLOW_INACTIVE)` (as in `tests/test1/test_authorization_matrix.py:424-429` and `tests/test1/test_guardian_parent_none.py:706-707`), so the request exercises the authenticated-inactive code path rather than duplicating the `anonymous` cell.
- **LOW — `SiblingContextButton` key naming on `GuardianListViewPermissionRequired` pages (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md:148, 155`):**
  - *Input:* A `SiblingContextButton` targeting a sibling Guardian `create` view rendered on a child `GuardianListViewPermissionRequired` page (`obj=None`).
  - *What happens:* In `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:332-335`, `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` computes `target_key = context_button.key_target if context_button and context_button.key_target else key`. Because `SiblingContextButton` leaves `key_target = None` (`src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:35, 266`), if the test names the button `key="create"`, `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` looks up `"create"` on `self.cv_viewset` and overwrites `ctx["cv_access"]` with the host ViewSet's create access.
  - *What should happen:* Use a distinct action key (e.g., `key="sibling_create"`) when registering the `SiblingContextButton` in Task `06.48-01-02` (and fix `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` in Plan 02/06; see Concern in `06.48-02` and `06.48-06`).

### 4. Suggestions
- In Task `06.48-01-02` (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-01-PLAN.md:139-152`), state explicitly that `inactive` client requests use `override_settings(AUTHENTICATION_BACKENDS=INACTIVE_BACKENDS)` and `client.force_login(actor, backend=ALLOW_INACTIVE)` so `request.user.is_authenticated` is `True` and `request.user.is_active` is `False`.

### 5. Risk Assessment
**LOW** — This plan modifies no files under `src/`, `docs/`, `CHANGELOG.md`, or `tests/test1/golden/`, and verifies that `git diff` against `dbf1e6c4` on those paths is empty.

## 06.48-02

### 1. Summary
`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md` introduces the private module `src/crud_views/lib/link_access.py` (`_link_permission`, `_link_access`, `_link_ctx`, `_higher_ancestor_gates`) and the private classmethod seam `_cv_link_permission` on `CrudView`, `GuardianQuerysetMixin`, and `GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired`. It wires two tracer call sites—the object navbar child item (`src/crud_views/lib/navbar.py:84-88`) and the nested Guardian list-page create override (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:328-357`)—and updates the navbar and Guardian list-page test expectations.

### 2. Strengths
- **Preserves `parent_spy` compatibility (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md:199-201, 224-228`):** Importing `_immediate_parent_access` inside `CrudView._cv_link_permission` and keeping the module-level import in `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py` and `views.py` ensures `parent_spy` in `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py:615-630` continues to intercept every immediate-parent gate call.
- **Preserves `view=` identity and trace/checker behavior (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md:124-136`):** Passing `rendering_view=None` at the navbar and `rendering_view=self` at `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` preserves the exact `_request_checker` caching (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:61-72`) and access-trace owner keys (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/ancestors.py:86`), keeping `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py` and `tests/test1/test_ancestor_costs.py` counts unchanged.
- **Thorough trace of C2 (`test_reached_policy_errors_propagate`) (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md:158-162`):** Accurately identifies that `tests/test1/test_navbar_permissions.py:167-207` with `allowed=False` previously rendered `""` only because `CrudView._cv_child_link_has_access` (`src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:530-531`) checked `cv_has_access` before `_immediate_parent_access`.

### 3. Concerns
- **MEDIUM — `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` button-class check (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md:237-241` and `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:332-337`):**
  - *Input:* A `ParentContextButton(key="parent_create", key_target="create")` or `SiblingContextButton(key="create", sibling_name="...", sibling_key="create")` rendered on a nested `GuardianListViewPermissionRequired` page (`obj=None`) whose own ViewSet also registers a `"create"` view.
  - *What happens:* `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:332-335` does:
    ```python
    context_button = self.cv_get_context_button(key)
    target_key = context_button.key_target if context_button and context_button.key_target else key
    if self.cv_viewset.is_view_registered(target_key):
        target_cls = self.cv_viewset.get_view_class(target_key)
    ```
    Because `ParentContextButton` and `SiblingContextButton` subclass `ContextButton` (`src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:132, 255`), `ParentContextButton` sets `key_target="create"` to refer to the *parent* ViewSet's create view (`buttons.py:153`), and `SiblingContextButton` has `key_target=None` so `target_key` falls back to `key`. `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` mistakes both for a `ContextButton` targeting `self.cv_viewset.get_view_class("create")` and overwrites `ctx` with `_link_ctx(_link_access(self_create_cls, user, None, (_resolved_parent(self),), rendering_view=self))`.
  - *What should happen:* Guard the `context_button` resolution in `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` (`src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:332-335`) so that if `context_button is not None and type(context_button).get_context is not ContextButton.get_context` (the same check used in `ContextButton.cv_check_target` at `src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:57`), `target_cls` is `None` and `GuardianQuerysetMixin.cv_get_context` does not overwrite the subclass button's context.
- **LOW — Actor check order vs configuration validation in `_higher_ancestor_gates` (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md:214-219`):**
  - *Input:* A two-level ancestor list `(book, publisher)` passed to `_link_access` with an anonymous or inactive actor when `target` has a malformed `ancestor_policy` (missing operation requirements, undeclared higher key, or non-default auth DB router).
  - *What happens:* `.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md:214-215` checks `not (user.is_authenticated and user.is_active)` and returns `False` *before* calling `ancestors._operation_requirements(target)`, `_check_nested_auth`, and `ancestors._permission_name`. By contrast, `_immediate_parent_access` (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:683-685`) and `_authorize_ancestors` (`src/crud_views/lib/ancestors.py:478-479`) validate operation requirements, auth DB routing, and permission names before denying an anonymous/inactive actor on a core ancestor.
  - *What should happen:* Consider running `_check_nested_auth`, `_operation_requirements`, and `_permission_name` before the actor-active check (or checking `(user.is_authenticated and user.is_active)` after the provider call, matching `CrudView._cv_link_permission` at `src/crud_views/lib/view/base.py:535-541`), and guard against `known_ancestors[index] is None`.

### 4. Suggestions
- In `src/crud_views_guardian/lib/mixins.py:332-335`, check `if context_button is not None and type(context_button).get_context is not ContextButton.get_context: target_key = None` so `ParentContextButton` and `SiblingContextButton` on a Guardian list page are never overwritten by the list view's own child-create logic.
- In `tests/test1/test_navbar_costs.py:150-152` (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-02-PLAN.md:163`), note explicitly for the executor that `spies[1]` (`ns.target._cv_link_permission`) has `resolved_owner` at `spy.call_args.args[2]` (`(current_actor, None, resolved_owner)`), whereas `spies[0]`, `spies[2]`, and `spies[3]` have `resolved_owner` at `spy.call_args.args[1]`.

### 5. Risk Assessment
**MEDIUM** — Introduces the core link-access check and per-target seam and rewires two production paths; risk is well-bounded by keeping the characterization matrix and cost suites green.

## 06.48-03

### 1. Summary
`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-03-PLAN.md` adds thirteen unit tests in `tests/test1/test_link_access.py` directly exercising `_link_access`, `_link_permission`, `_link_ctx`, `_higher_ancestor_gates`, and `_cv_link_permission` across all stage-1 decisions (D-04, D-05, D-06, D-07, D-13, D-16, D-17, D-18, D-19, D-20, D-26, D-31, D-32), and updates `tests/test1/test_navbar_checks.py:137-141` to also guard `cv_action_enabled` during system checks.

### 2. Strengths
- **Direct verification of multi-level ancestor gating (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-03-PLAN.md:139-143`):** `test_ancestors_two_level_list_gates_every_level` and `test_ancestors_unprotected_parent_moves_nearest_key_up` directly prove D-04, D-07, and D-26 using the existing three-level `helpers` fixture from `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:37-114`.
- **Hardens the startup system-check guard (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-03-PLAN.md:208-213`):** In `tests/test1/test_navbar_checks.py:137-141`, `test_invalid_configurations` previously only patched `cv_has_access` and `_cv_action_enabled_for_rendering` with `pytest.fail`. Since `navbar._build` now calls `cv_action_enabled` directly for child items, also patching `cv_action_enabled` preserves the test's guarantee.

### 3. Concerns
- **LOW — Distinguishing `nearest` vs `higher` key in `test_ancestors_unprotected_parent_moves_nearest_key_up` (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-03-PLAN.md:142-143`):**
  - *Input:* `test_ancestors_unprotected_parent_moves_nearest_key_up` with `open_book` (`provider="none"`) between `pub` and `open_note` (`tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:83-91`).
  - *What happens:* For `helpers.open_note.List`, the default `Read` pair is `AncestorPermissions(nearest="view", higher="view")` (`src/crud_views/lib/settings.py:92`), so `nearest` and `higher` are both `"view"`. If the test only checks `helpers.open_note.List` without overriding `read`, a bug that used `higher` instead of `nearest` on `Publisher` would still check `"app.view_publisher"` and pass.
  - *What should happen:* Use `helpers.open_note.Create` (where `Write` has `nearest="change"` and `higher="view"`) or a custom `Read` pair with `nearest != higher`, and assert in `provider_calls` that `Publisher` was checked for `"app.change_publisher"` (`nearest`), not `"app.view_publisher"` (`higher`).

### 4. Suggestions
- In `test_ancestors_unprotected_parent_moves_nearest_key_up` (`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-03-PLAN.md:142-143`), test `helpers.open_note.Create` (or both `List` with a distinct `nearest` key and `Create`) and assert `provider_calls == [(helpers.pub.vs.name, "app.change_publisher", data.penguin)]`.

### 5. Risk Assessment
**LOW** — Pure unit-test and system-check-guard additions with a strict rule to stop on any unexpected matrix or cost change.

## 06.48-04

### 1. Summary
`.planning/phases/06.48-link-access-check-as-a-private-function-narrow-obj-in-cv-has/06.48-04-PLAN.md` moves `ChildContextButton.get_context` (`src/crud_views/lib/view/buttons.py:222-252`) and the child branch of `CardListView.cv_get_card_action_data` (`src/crud_views/lib/views/card.py:57-74`) to `_link_access`, deletes all three definitions of `_cv_child_link_has_access` (`CrudView`, `GuardianQuerysetMixin`, `GuardianCreateViewPermissionRequired`), updates the four D-25 lines in `tests/test1/test_ancestor_helpers.py:217-219, 355`, and reconciles `tests/test1/test_guardian_child_link_visibility.py` and `tests/test1/test_core_nested_create_state.py`.

### 2. Strengths
